.

The following
appendix

ensures that the parallel execution of strongly swappable transactions
is equivalent to any sequential execution of them.

Theorem 25
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}
be strongly swappable w.r.t.
\#^{\mathtt{W}}_{\mathtt{R}}
, and
let
{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}
.
Then, for all
\sigma
:

\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}\;=\;\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}

Proof B.8
.

Direct by Lemma
24
and
by the fact that every
\mathtt{W}
is compatible with
\Pi^{\star}{}
.

A
parellelized blockchain

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}

is a finite sequence of multisets of transactions;
we denote with
{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}
the empty sequence.
We extend the semantics of multisets (
Appendix

B
) to
parallelized blockchains as follows.

{defi}

[Semantics of parallelized blockchains]

The semantics of parallelized blockchains is defined as follows:

\mbox{$\llbracket{{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi}_{\sigma}$}=\sigma\qquad\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi}_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi}_{\scriptsize\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi}_{\sigma}$}}$}

We write
\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi}
for
\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi}_{\sigma_{0}}
,
where
\sigma_{0}
is the initial state.

We also extend the serialization relation
\vartriangleleft
(Definition
B
) to
parallelized blockchains.

{defi}

[Serialization of parallelized blockchains]
We define the relation
\vartriangleleft
between blockchains and
parallelized blockchains as follows:

\dfrac{}{{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}\vartriangleleft{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}}\qquad\qquad\dfrac{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}\quad{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{2}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{2}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}

The following
appendix

states that our technique to parallelize the transactions in a blockchain
preserves its semantics.

Theorem 26
.

Let each multiset in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}
be strongly swappable w.r.t.
\#^{\mathtt{W}}_{\mathtt{R}}
, and
let
{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}
.
Then, for all
\sigma
:

\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}\;=\;\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}

Proof B.9
.

By induction on the rule used for deriving
{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}
.

•

Rule:
\dfrac{}{{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}\vartriangleleft{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}}
.

The thesis follows trivially, since

\mbox{$\llbracket{{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}=\sigma=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}
.

•

Rule:
\dfrac{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}\quad{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{2}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{2}}\vartriangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}
.

By Theorem
25
, for some reachable
\sigma^{\prime}

it must be

\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}=\sigma^{\prime}=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}
.
By the induction hypothesis,

\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{2}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma^{\prime}}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}$}
.
The thesis then follows by:

\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{2}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma^{\prime}}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{2}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}\qquad\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{B}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}

Lemma 27
.

Let

{\sf N}_{\mathcal{R}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{n}})=({\sf P},{\sf Tr},{\sf{F}},{\sf{m}_{0}})
.
Then
({\sf Tr},<^{*})
is a partial order.

Proof B.10
.

Transitivity and reflexivity hold by definition.
For antisymmetricity, assume that

({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{i}},i)<^{*}({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{j}},j)
and
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{j}},j)<^{*}({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{i}},i)
.
Then, it is easy to verify that
i\leq j
and
j\leq i
, and so
i=j
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{j}}
are uniquely determined by
i
and
j
, we have
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{j}}
.
Therefore,
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{i}},i)=({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{j}},j)
, as required.

See
14

Proof B.11
.

By Definition
4
, the first three conditions
of the definition of occurrence net are easy to verify.
To prove that
{\sf{F}}^{*}
is acyclic, we proceed by contradiction.
Assume that there is a sequence

\boldsymbol{x}=x_{0},x_{1},\ldots x_{m}
such that
(x_{i},x_{i+1})\in{\sf{F}}

for all
0\leq i<m
, and
x_{0}=x_{m}
with
m>0
.
Notice that the above sequence alternates between
transitions and places, and so, since
m>0
,
at least one place and one transition occur in
\boldsymbol{x}
.
Further, a place between two transitions

\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
can exist only if
\mathsf{t}<{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
.
Therefore, if
\mathsf{t},{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
occur in
\boldsymbol{x}
,
it must be
\mathsf{t}<^{*}{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
and
{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}<^{*}\mathsf{t}
.
So, if
\boldsymbol{x}
contains at least two transitions, by Lemma
27
,
we have a contradiction. If only one transition
\mathsf{t}=({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)
occurs in
\boldsymbol{x}
,
then there is a place of the form
(\mathsf{t},\mathsf{t})
occuring in
\boldsymbol{x}
.
Therefore,
\mathsf{t}<\mathsf{t}
, which implies
i<i
— contradiction.

Lemma 28
.

Let
\sf{N}=({\sf P},{\sf Tr},{\sf{F}},{\sf{m}_{0}})
be an occurrence net.
For all
\mathsf{t},{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}\in{\sf Tr}
, if
\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
then

{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}\cap{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}=\emptyset
.

Proof B.12
.

By contradiction, assume that
{\sf{p}}\in{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}\cap{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}

with
\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
.
Then,
\{\mathsf{t},{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}\}\subseteq{{\sf{p}}}{{}^{\bullet}}
, and hence

|{{\sf{p}}}{{}^{\bullet}}|\geq 2
—
contradiction with constraint
1

of the definition of occurrence nets.

Lemma 29
.

Let
{\sf{m}}
be a reachable marking of an occurrence net
\sf{N}
.
Then:

(1)

If
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
and
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
,
then
{\sf{m}^{\prime}}={\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
(determinism).

(2)

If
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
,
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}

and
\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
, then there exists
{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that

{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}

(diamond property).

(3)

If
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}\;\xrightarrow{}^{*}\,\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}

then
\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}

(linearity).

(4)

If
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}}{\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}}
then
|\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}|=0

(acyclicity).

Proof B.13
.

For item
1
,
by definition of the firing of transitions of Petri Nets it must be

{\sf{m}^{\prime}}={\sf{m}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}+{\mathsf{t}}{{}^{\bullet}}={\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
.

For item
2
,
since
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
and
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
,
it must be:

\displaystyle{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}\subseteq{\sf{m}}

\displaystyle{\sf{m}^{\prime}}={\sf{m}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}+{\mathsf{t}}{{}^{\bullet}}

\displaystyle{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}\subseteq{\sf{m}}

\displaystyle{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}={\sf{m}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}+{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{{}^{\bullet}}

By Lemma
28
,

{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
is enabled at
{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
, and
\mathsf{t}
is enabled at
{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
.
Then, by definition of firing:

{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}+{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{{}^{\bullet}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}+{\mathsf{t}}{{}^{\bullet}}

Then:

\displaystyle{\sf{m}^{\prime}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}+{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{{}^{\bullet}}

\displaystyle=({\sf{m}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}+{\mathsf{t}}{{}^{\bullet}})-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}+{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{{}^{\bullet}}

\displaystyle=({\sf{m}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}+{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{{}^{\bullet}})-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}+{\mathsf{t}}{{}^{\bullet}}

(as
{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}\subseteq{\sf{m}}
)

\displaystyle={\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{\mathsf{t}}+{\mathsf{t}}{{}^{\bullet}}

Hence, the thesis follows by choosing

{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}={\sf{m}^{\prime}}-{\rm Phys.~Rev.~E}{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}+{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{{}^{\bullet}}
.

Item
3
follows directly
by induction on the length of the reduction
\xrightarrow{}^{*}
,
exploiting the fact that
{\sf{F}}^{*}
is a partial order.

Item
4
follows by the fact that

{\sf{F}}^{*}
is a partial order.

Lemma 30
.

Let
\sf{N}=({\sf P},{\sf Tr},{\sf{F}},{\sf{m}_{0}})
be an occurrence net,
and let
{\sf{m}}
be a reachable marking, such that,
for some
\mathsf{t}
,
{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
,
{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\!\!}{}^{n+1}\;\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}}
/\penalty
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}}

Then,
{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{}^{n}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
.

Proof B.14
.

By induction on
n
.
For the base case, it must be
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{}^{1}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
,
and hence
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
for some
{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
. Since
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
, by the contrapositive of
item
2
of Lemma
29

(diamond property) it follows
\mathsf{t}={{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
.
So, by item
1

of Lemma
29

(determinism) we have that
{\sf{m}^{\prime}}={\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
. Clearly:

{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{}^{0}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}

For the induction case, let
n=m+1
, for some
m
.
Then, for some
{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}},{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
:

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{}^{m+1}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}

If
\mathsf{t}={{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
, then by
item
1

of Lemma
29
(determinism)
it follows that
{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}={\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
,
and so we have the thesis
{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{}^{n}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
.
Otherwise, if
\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
,
by item
2

of Lemma
29
(diamond property),
there must exists

{\sf{m}_{1}}
such that:

{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}{\sf{m}_{1}}

We are in the following situation:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf{m}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\!\!}{}^{m+1}\;\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;{\sf{m}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}}
/\penalty
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}}

Since
m+1=n
, by the induction hypothesis:

{\sf{m}_{1}}\xrightarrow{}^{m}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}

Therefore, we have the thesis:

{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}\xrightarrow{}^{m}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}

Lemma 31
.

Let
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i),({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)
be transitions of

{\sf N}_{\mathcal{R}}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}})
.
If
{\sf{m}}
is a reachable marking, then:

({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)<({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)\;\;\text{and}\;\;{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)}\quad\implies\quad{\sf{m}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 13.95055pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 13.95055pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 11.65617pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 11.65617pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)}}}}

Proof B.15
.

By the construction in Figure
4
,
since
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)<({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)
, then

{\sf{p}}=(({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i),({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j))
is a place of the occurrence net,
and

{\sf{F}}(({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i),{\sf{p}})=1
and
{\sf{F}}({\sf{p}},({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j))=1
.

{defi}

[Independency]

Let
\sf{N}
be an occurrence net.
We say that two transitions
\mathsf{t}
and
{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
are
independent
,
in symbols
\mathsf{t}\;\mathrm{I}\;{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
,
if
\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
and there exists a reachable marking
{\sf{m}}
such that:

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}

We define
\simeq

as the least congruence in the free monoid
{\sf Tr}^{*}
such that,
for all
\mathsf{t},{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}\in{\sf Tr}
:

\;\mathsf{t}\,I\,{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}\implies\mathsf{t}{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}\simeq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}\mathsf{t}
.

Lemma 32
.

Let
\sf{N}
be an occurrence net, with a reachable marking
{\sf{m}}
.
If
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{{\sf U}}
then
\mathsf{t}\;\mathrm{I}\;{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}
,
for all
\mathsf{t}\neq{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}\in{\sf U}
.

Proof B.16
.

Since
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{{\sf U}}
, then
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}}
for all

\mathsf{t}\in{\sf U}
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
\sf{N}
be an occurrence net,
and let
{\sf{m}}
be a reachable marking.
If
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}}{\!}^{*}\,{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
and

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}}{\!}^{*}\,{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
,
then
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}\simeq\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}
.

Proof B.17
.

We proceed by induction on the length of the longest reduction among

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}}{\!}^{*}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
and

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}}{\!}^{*}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
.
For the base case, the thesis is trivial as both
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}
and

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}
are empty.
For the induction case, assume that
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}

is longer or equal to
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}
(the other case is symmetric).
Let
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}=\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}
.
We first show that
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}
is not empty.
By contradiction, if
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}
is empty, then
{\sf{m}}={\sf{m}^{\prime}}
.
But then, by item
4
of
Lemma
29
(acyclicity) it follows that
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}
is
empty as well: contradiction.
Therefore,
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}=\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}
for some
\mathsf{t}_{2}
and

\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}
.
Clearly,
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}
is longer
than
\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}
and
\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}
.
Let
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{1}}{\sf{m}_{1}}
and
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{2}}{\sf{m}_{2}}
.
We have two subcases.

•

If
\mathsf{t}_{1}=\mathsf{t}_{2}
,
by determinism (Lemma
29
)
it follows that
{\sf{m}_{1}}={\sf{m}_{2}}
.
Let
{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}={\sf{m}_{1}}
.
By the hypothesis of the
appendix
, we have

{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}}{\!\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
and

{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}}{\!\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
.
Then, by the induction hypothesis we have

\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}\simeq\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}
,
and so the thesis
\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}\simeq\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}

follows since
\simeq
is a congruence.

•

If
\mathsf{t}_{1}\neq\mathsf{t}_{2}
, then by Definition
B
it must be

\mathsf{t}_{1}\;\mathrm{I}\;\mathsf{t}_{2}
.
By the diamond property (Lemma
29
),
there exists
{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}

such that
{\sf{m}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{2}}{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
and

{\sf{m}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{1}}{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}
.
By linearity
(item
3
of Lemma
29
),

{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 9.2621pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{1}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 9.2621pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{1}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 8.901pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{1}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 8.901pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{1}}}}}
and

{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 9.2621pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 9.2621pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 8.901pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 8.901pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}
.
By Lemma
30
, applied on
{\sf{m}_{2}}
,
there exists
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}
such that

{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}}{\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}

and
|\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}|+1=|\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}|
.
So, we are in the following situation:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf{m}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf{m}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf{m}^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;{\sf{m}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sf{m}^{\prime}}}
/\penalty
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 8.19447pt\mathsf{t}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{t}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}}
/\penalty
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 8.19447pt\mathsf{t}_{1}}

Therefore, we have that:

\displaystyle{\sf{m}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}}{\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf{m}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}}{\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle{\sf{m}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}}{\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf{m}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}}{\!\!}^{*}\;{\sf{m}^{\prime}}

Notice that

|\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}|=|\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}|=|\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}|+1=|\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}|\leq|\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}|<|\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}|
.
Hence, by applying the induction hypothesis twice:

\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}

Then, since
\simeq
is a congruence:

\mathsf{t}_{1}\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}

Since
\mathsf{t}_{1}\;\mathrm{I}\;\mathsf{t}_{2}
,
then
\mathsf{t}_{1}\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\mathsf{t}_{2}\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}
.
By transitivity of
\simeq
:

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}\;=\;\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}\;\simeq\;\mathsf{t}_{1}\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\;\simeq\;\mathsf{t}_{2}\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\;\simeq\;\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}\;=\;\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}

{defi}

For all sequences of transitions
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}
, we define
the set
{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}})
of the transitions
occurring in
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}
as:

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}})=\left\{{\mathsf{t}}\,\middle|\,{\exists\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}},\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}:\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}=\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}\mathsf{t}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}}\right\}

and we extend
{\it tr}
to step firing sequences
\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}
as follows:

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}})=\bigcup\left\{{{\sf U}}\,\middle|\,{\exists\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}},\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}:\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}=\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}}{\sf U}\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}}\right\}

Lemma 34
.

If
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}
then

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}})={\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}})
.

Proof B.18
.

Trivial by Definition
B
.

Lemma 35
.

Let
\sf{N}
be an occurrence net,
and let
{\sf{m}}
be a reachable marking.
If
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}
,

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}}{\sf{m}_{2}}
and

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}}})={\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}})
, then

{\sf{m}_{1}}={\sf{m}_{2}}
.

Proof B.19
.

Since
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}
and

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}}{\sf{m}_{2}}
, there exist
sequentialisations
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}
of
\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}}

and
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}
of
\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}
such that

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}
and

{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}}{\sf{m}_{2}}
.
Since by hypothesis

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}}})={\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}})
, then

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}})={\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}})
.
We proceed by induction on the length of
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}
.
The base case is trivial, as
{\sf{m}}={\sf{m}_{1}}={\sf{m}_{2}}
.
For the inductive case, suppose
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}=\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}
,
with
|\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}|=n
.
By determinism, there exists a unique marking
{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{1}}

such that
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{1}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{1}}
(a single step).
Since
{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{1}}})={\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}_{2}}})
, it must be
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}=\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}
,
with
|\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime}}}|=n
.
Let
{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{2}}
be the unique
marking such that
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{2}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{2}}
(a single step).

There are two subcases.

•

If
\mathsf{t}_{1}=\mathsf{t}_{2}
, then
{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{1}}={\sf{m}^{\prime}_{2}}
, and so
the thesis follows directly by the induction hypothesis.

•

If
\mathsf{t}_{1}\neq\mathsf{t}_{2}
,
by the diamond property
(item
1
of Lemma
29
),
there exists
{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
such that
{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{2}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
and

{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{1}}{\sf{m}^{\prime}}
. Since
\mathsf{t}_{2}\in{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}})
, by linearity
(item
3
of Lemma
29
)
it follows that

{\sf{m}_{1}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 9.2621pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 9.2621pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 8.901pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 8.901pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\xrightarrow{\;\;\mathsf{t}_{2}}}}}
, and hence,
by applying Lemma
30
on
{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{1}}

we obtain
{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}

for some
\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}
. Summing up, we have that:

{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\sf{m}^{\prime}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}}{\sf{m}_{1}}

Then, by Lemma
33
,

\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}\simeq\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}
,
and hence:

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}=\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime}}}\simeq\mathsf{t}_{1}\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}

By Lemma
34
:

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}})={\it tr}({\mathsf{t}_{1}\mathsf{t}_{2}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}})

Similarly, we can conclude that
{\sf{m}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}}{\sf{m}_{2}}

for some
\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}
and that:

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}})={\it tr}({\mathsf{t}_{2}\mathsf{t}_{1}\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}})

Since
{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}})={\it tr}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{2}}})
,
we can conclude:

{\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}})={\it tr}({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}})

Since
|\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}|=n-1<n+1=|\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}_{1}}|
,
the thesis follows by the induction hypothesis.

Lemma 36
.

Let
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)
and
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)
be transitions of
{\sf N}_{\mathcal{R}}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}})
.
Then:

({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)\;\mathrm{I}\;({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)\quad\implies\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}\mathcal{R}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}}

Proof B.20
.

By Definition
B
,

({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)\;\mathrm{I}\;({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)
implies that

({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)\neq({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)
and there exists some reachable marking

{\sf{m}}
such that
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)}
and
{\sf{m}}\xrightarrow{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)}
.
By contradiction, assume that
\neg({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}\mathcal{R}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}})
.
Then, since
i<j
or
j>i
, by Definition
4

we would have that
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)<({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)
or
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)<({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)
.
Then, by Lemma
31

we obtain a contradiction.

{defi}

Let
{\sf N}_{\mathcal{R}}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}})=({\sf P},{\sf Tr},{\sf{F}},{\sf{m}_{0}})
.
We define
\alpha:{\sf Tr}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\mathbb{T}}}
as

\alpha({{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i})={\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}
.
We then extend
\alpha
to a function from steps to
multisets of transactions as follows:

\alpha({\emptyset})=[]\qquad\alpha({{\sf U}\cup\{\mathsf{t}\}})=[{\alpha({\mathsf{t}})}]+\alpha({{\sf U}})\;\;

Finally, we extend
\alpha
to finite sequences of steps as follows:

\alpha({{\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}})={\mathbfsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\varepsilon}}}\qquad\alpha({{\sf U}\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}})=\alpha({{\sf U}})\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}})

Lemma 37
.

Let
{\sf N}_{\mathcal{R}}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}})=({\sf P},{\sf Tr},{\sf{F}},{\sf{m}_{0}})
,
and let
\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}
be a step firing sequence.
Then, for all
\Pi
and
\sigma
:

\mbox{$\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi}_{\sigma}$}\;=\;\mbox{$\llbracket{\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}})}\rrbracket^{\Pi}_{\sigma}$}

Proof B.21
.

Straightforward by Definitions
4
and
B
.

Lemma 38
.

If
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}
holds in
{\sf N}_{\mathcal{R}}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}})
, then

\alpha({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}})\simeq_{\mathcal{R}}\alpha({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}})
.

Proof B.22
.

Define:

\simeq^{\prime}\;=\;\left\{{(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}},\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}})}\,\middle|\,{\alpha({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}})\simeq_{\mathcal{R}}\alpha({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}})}\right\}

It suffices to show that
\simeq\;\subseteq\;\simeq^{\prime}
.
Notice that
\simeq^{\prime}
is a congruence satisfying:

{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}\mathcal{R}{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}}\;\;\implies\;\;({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)\simeq^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)

But then, by Lemma
36
,
it follows that
\simeq^{\prime}
also satisfies:

({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)\;\mathrm{I}\;({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)\;\;\implies\;\;({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)\simeq^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T^{\prime}}},j)({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}},i)

Since
\simeq
is the smallest congruence satisfying
this implication, we have

\simeq\;\subseteq\;\simeq^{\prime}
.

See
16

Proof B.23
.

For item
1
,
assume that
{\sf{m}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}}{\sf{m}}
and

{\sf{m}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}}{\sf{m}}
.
A standard result from Petri nets theory ensures that
there exists sequentializations
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}
of
\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}

and
\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}
of
\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}
such that:

{\sf{m}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}}{\sf{m}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf{m}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}}{\sf{m}}

By Lemma
33
,
it must be
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}\simeq\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}
.
Then, by Lemma
38
:

\alpha({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}})\simeq_{\#^{\mathtt{W}}_{\mathtt{R}}}\alpha({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}})

By Lemma
17
:

\mbox{$\llbracket{\alpha({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}})}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{\alpha({\boldsymbol{{{\sf{t}}^{\prime}}}})}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}

By Lemmas
32
and
36
,
it follows that all multisets of transactions in
\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}})
,
as well as those in
\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}})
,
are strongly swappable w.r.t.
\#^{\mathtt{W}}_{\mathtt{R}}
.
Therefore, by Theorem
26
:

\mbox{$\llbracket{\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}})}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}})}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}

Then, by Lemma
37
:

\mbox{$\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}

For item
2
, note that a transition
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{i}},i)

is enabled if all transitions
({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{j}},j)
with
j<i
have been fired.
So
\{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}},1)\}\cdots\{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{n}},n)\}
is a step firing sequence. Moreover,
\{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}},1)\}\cdots\{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{n}},n)\}

contains all the transactions of
{\sf N}_{\#^{\mathtt{W}}_{\mathtt{R}}}({\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}})
, and so, by linearity (Lemma
3
) it is maximal.

For item
3
,
let
\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}=\{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{1}},1)\}\cdots\{({\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{\sf T}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{n}},n)\}
. By item
2
, we have that
\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}
is a maximal
step firing sequence. It is easy to see that
{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}\vartriangleleft\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}})
.
By Theorem
26
:

\mbox{$\llbracket{\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}})}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}

Since
\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}
and
\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}
are both maximal, by
Lemma
35
and
by item
1
, it follows that:

\mbox{$\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}

Since
\mbox{$\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{\alpha({\boldsymbol{{\sf U^{\prime}}}})}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}

(by Lemma
37
) we have that:

\mbox{$\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\sf U}}}\rrbracket^{\Pi^{\star}}_{\sigma}$}=\mbox{$\llbracket{{\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0.2,0.4}{B}}}}}\rrbracket_{\sigma}$}

A simple ERC-721 token implementation

⬇

pragma

solidity

>=

0.4.2;

contract

Token

{

mapping
(
uint256

=>

address
)

owner
;

mapping
(
uint256

=>

address
)

approved
;

mapping
(
uint256

=>

bool
)

exists
;

mapping
(
address

=>

uint256
)

balance
;

mapping

(
address

=>

mapping

(
address

=>

bool
))

opApprovals
;

function

ownerOf
(
uint256

tkId
)

external

view

returns

(
address
)

{

require

(
exists
[
tkId
]);

require

(
owner
[
tkId
]

!=

address
(0));

return

owner
[
tkId
];

}

function

balanceOf
(
address

addr
)

external

view

returns

(
uint256
)

{

require

(
addr

!=

address
(0));

return

balance
[
addr
];

}

function

approve
(
address

addr
,

uint256

tkId
)

external

{

require

(
exists
[
tkId
]);

require

(
owner
[
tkId
]

==

msg
.
sender

&&

addr

!=

msg
.
sender
);

approved
[
tkId
]

=

addr
;

}

function

setApprovalForAll
(
address

op
,

bool

isApproved
)

external

{

operatorApprovals
[
msg
.
sender
][
op
]

=

isApproved
;

}

function

getApproved
(
uint256

tkId
)

external

view

returns

(
address
)

{

require

(
exists
[
tkId
]);

return

approved
[
tkId
];

}

function

isApprovedForAll
(
address

addr
,

address

op
)

external

view

returns

(
bool
)

{

return

operatorApprovals
[
addr
][
op
];

}

function

transferFrom
(
address

from
,

address

to
,

uint256

tkId
)

external

{

require

(
exists
[
tkId
]);

require

(
from

==

owner
[
tkId
]

&&

from

!=

to
);

require

(
to

!=

address
(0));

if

(
from

==

msg
.
sender

||

operatorApprovals
[
from
][
msg
.
sender
]

||

approved
[
tkId
]

==

msg
.
sender
)

{

owner
[
tkId
]

=

to
;

approved
[
tkId
]

=

address
(0);

balance
[
from
]

-=

1;

balance
[
to
]

+=

1;

}

}

function

mint
(
address

to
,

uint256

tkId
)

external

{

require

(!
exists
[
tkId
]);

require

(
to

!=

address
(0));

exists
[
tkId
]

=

true
;

owner
[
tkId
]

=

to
;

balance
[
to
]

+=

1;

}

}
